# 审批明细互通合同——符号锚点表(附录)

生成:2026-09-04(v14 按 §0 规范句优先规则重分类) · 合同版本:v14 · 锚点 SHA:`5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba`(origin/main) · 被审文本:`d5c5606d84`(v11)

统计:316 条可核断言;判定 TRUE 231 · FALSE 51 · STALE 1 · UNCLEAR 3 · NOT_A_MAIN_CLAIM 30。
处置:①已更正 19 · ②规范性目标 58 · ③分类产物 8(非 TRUE 行全部归类,无第四类;③行在处置列引用被拆错的原句)。

**读法**:①=合同曾把过时/错误事实当前提,v12/v13 正文已逐条更正(id:axioms-07, axioms-09, axioms-15, tail-07, tail-11, v11-a-03, v11-a-40, v11-a-41, v2-addr-26, v2-addr-40, v2-claim-22, v2-claim-47, v2-gate1-01, v2-gate11-02, v2-gate2-05, v2-gate2-09, v2-gate3-08, v2-gate6-02, v2-pos-02);
②=合同原句为规范句(须/必须/应/不得/目标/v1.1),抽取器误当现状核,判 FALSE 只说明目标尚未实现;
③=抽取器把**事实句**拆错后核出的产物,合同原文正确。本表按 SHA 冻结;ratify 前须对最新 main 重生成(§0 规程)。anchors 为 file:line@SHA,values-free,观察列不截断。

| axioms-01 | §1 | TRUE | — | Submitting an approval instance persists an initial form_snapshot on the approval_instances row. | packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:8021@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:8026@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:8059@5133df1c5 | The instance-create path's single `INSERT INTO approval_instances (... form_snapshot ...)` statement writes `JSON.stringify(normalizedFormData)` into the form_snapshot column as part of the row's initial insert (params $15/$1..$17 by position; form_snapshot is column 21 of the list, bound to the JSON.stringify(normalizedFormData) argument). |
| axioms-02 | §1 | TRUE | — | While an instance is status='pending', an authorized handler node can update approval_instances.form_snapshot and append a field revision row inside the same DB transaction. | packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10262@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10363@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:12297@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10437@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10439@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10535@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10559@5133df1c5 | dispatchAction only reaches the 'handle' action branch when the row-locked (`FOR UPDATE`) instance has status==='pending' (guard at :10262). Inside that same transaction/client, applyHandlerFieldWrites is called (:10363), which UPDATEs approval_instances.form_snapshot in place (:12297); its returned revisions are then appended via insertFormFieldRevisions (INSERT INTO approval_form_field_revisions) either on the partial-handle branch (:10437) before that branch's own COMMIT (:10439), or on the completion branch (:10535) before the completion COMMIT (:10559) — always the same client, never a separate transaction. |
| axioms-03 | §1 | TRUE | — | The approval terminal-state transaction commits the final snapshot, the status transition to 'approved', and the completion event emission in one transaction. | packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:12297@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10363@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10484@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10540@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10556@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10559@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:11098@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:11223@5133df1c5 | Scope matters here: only on the handler-submit ('handle') path that carries fieldWrites and whose resolution completes the workflow does one transaction contain all three — the form_snapshot UPDATE (applyHandlerFieldWrites called at :10363, UPDATE at :12297), the status UPDATE to resolution.status (:10484, 'approved' when terminal), and the in-transaction completion-event build+durable-enqueue (:10540-10556) — before COMMIT (:10559). On the plain 'approve' action path (no handler field write in that transaction), the terminal transaction still commits the status→'approved' UPDATE (:11098-11112) and the completion-event enqueue (:11201-11216) together before COMMIT (:11223), but it performs no form_snapshot write at all — the snapshot's most recent write already happened and committed in an earlier, separate transaction (create-time INSERT or a prior handle-round's UPDATE). |
| axioms-04 | §1 | TRUE | — | A status guard exists that rejects handler-node form_snapshot writes once the instance is in a terminal state. | packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:9540@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:9552@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10262@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10363@5133df1c5 | dispatchAction (:9540) SELECTs the instance FOR UPDATE (:9552), then throws a 409 APPROVAL_ERROR_CODES.INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION when `instance.status !== 'pending'` (:10262-10267) — this check runs before the 'handle' branch (which is what calls applyHandlerFieldWrites at :10363) can be reached. The condition rejects any non-'pending' status, which is a strict superset of "terminal" (approved/rejected are both covered, along with any other non-pending value the schema may carry). |
| axioms-05 | §1 | TRUE | — | The FWB (form writeback) executor only reads form_snapshot from instances whose status='approved'. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-write-action.ts:180@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3761@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3882@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3903@5133df1c5 | executeWriteApprovalFormValues itself (approval-fwb-write-action.ts:180) receives formValues as an input parameter and issues no approval_instances query of its own. The status gate lives in its two production callers in automation-executor.ts: the create-path SELECT (`SELECT form_snapshot FROM approval_instances WHERE ... AND status = 'approved' FOR SHARE`, :3760-3761) and the update-path's writer-fence target-discovery SELECT (:3882) plus its main snapshot SELECT (:3900-3903), both carrying `... AND i.status = 'approved'`. A repo-wide, non-test grep for `form_snapshot` under packages/core-backend/src finds no other `SELECT form_snapshot FROM approval_instances`-shaped read, so these are the only two sites that source values for FWB, and both are gated on status='approved'. |
| axioms-06 | §1 A1 | TRUE | — | Embedded premise: the current handler form_snapshot UPDATE SQL does not itself carry a status='pending' predicate with an affected-rows==1 check; freezing today relies on a caller-side status check. | packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:12293@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:12296@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:12300@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts:10262@5133df1c5 | The applyHandlerFieldWrites UPDATE statement (`UPDATE approval_instances SET form_snapshot = COALESCE(form_snapshot,'{}'::jsonb) \|\| $2::jsonb, updated_at = now() WHERE id = $1`, :12296-12300) carries only a `WHERE id = $1` predicate — no `status = 'pending'` clause — and the code never inspects the query result's rowCount/affected-rows afterward. The only enforcement that a write happens while status='pending' is the caller-side check in dispatchAction (:10262), which runs before applyHandlerFieldWrites is invoked, on a row already locked by an earlier `FOR UPDATE` SELECT in the same transaction. |
| axioms-07 | §1 A1 | FALSE | ①已更正(正文 v12/v13) | Implied premise: a row_index concept/column already exists for detail-field rows in the approval domain (field revisions / snapshot rows). | packages/core-backend/src/db/migrations/zzzz20260817130000_create_approval_form_field_revisions.ts:20@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalGraphExecutor.ts:848@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalGraphExecutor.ts:849@5133df1c5 | No `row_index` column exists anywhere in the approval domain. approval_form_field_revisions' DDL (id, instance_id, node_key, field_id, before_value, after_value, actor_id, node_entry_epoch, audit_record_id, created_at) has no row_index column — a revision row is keyed by top-level field_id only, recording a detail field's entire array as one before/after JSONB value, never one row per detail-row. The single `rowIndex` occurrence in approval-domain source (ApprovalGraphExecutor.ts:848-849) is a transient `value.forEach((row, rowIndex) => ...)` loop variable used only to build a validation-error-message prefix (`${field.id}[${rowIndex}]`); it is never persisted or used as an identity. Every other `rowIndex`/`row_index` hit in the repo is either the unrelated legacy spreadsheet-grid tables (`spreadsheet_grid_cells.row_index`) or transient Vue list-rendering indices in apps/web. |
| axioms-08 | §1 A1 | TRUE | — | No real-time bidirectional sync between approval instances and multitable records exists in the codebase. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-record-projection-service.ts:8@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-record-projection-service.ts:9@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-record-projection-service.ts:82@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3761@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3903@5133df1c5 | A repo-wide census of approval_instances writers (`UPDATE approval_instances` / kysely `updateTable('approval_instances')`) finds none inside packages/core-backend/src/multitable — every writer is either a one-off DB migration (org_id backfills) or lives in routes/approvals.ts, ApprovalBridgeService.ts, or ApprovalProductService.ts's own action-dispatch code. The multitable subsystem only ever reads form_snapshot (status='approved'-gated, the FWB executor) to seed a new/updated multitable record once, at approval completion — a one-shot forward write, not a continuous two-way sync. A separate mechanism, approval-record-projection-service.ts (T3-6), independently documents and enforces the same one-way direction for its own approval-instance read-model projection: "a one-way projection, never a write-back (editing the projected record never mutates the approval)" (:8-9), with its allowed-column list explicitly excluding form_snapshot (:82). No code path writes any approval_instances column in response to a multitable record change. |
| axioms-09 | §1 A2 | FALSE | ①已更正(正文 v12/v13) | Exactly three interop seams exist today: record-link fields (table→approval reference), FWB (approval→table after approval), and the automation bridge (table events launching approvals). | docs/development/approval-form-writeback-fwb0-designlock-20260712.md:79-90@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-record-projection-service.ts:2-10@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/index.ts:3521-3529@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-service.ts:3320-3335@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:2540-2551@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/types/approval-product.ts:191@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba | The FWB-0 lock's own §0 (fwb0.md:79-90) names two OTHER existing table<->approval lines besides FWB, in a table: 'W7 resultWriteback' (T3-5, SHIPPED — approval result metadata written onto the triggering/specified record) and 'T3-6 approvals-as-records (投影/projection)' (already landed — each approval instance materialized as a row in a system-managed multitable sheet). Both are live, non-flag-gated code at this SHA: resultWriteback is validated/executed config on the start_approval action (automation-service.ts:390-475, :3320-3389, called 'start_approval action's resultWriteback') and is reasonably read as the return leg of the SAME automation-bridge seam. T3-6 is a fully separate subsystem with its own service file (approval-record-projection-service.ts, header: 'T3-6 — Approval data as first-class multitable records (read-model projection)'), its own migration (approval_record_projection table), its own consumer_key ('approval-projection' in automation-durable-activation.ts), and its own subscription at startup (index.ts:3521, subscribed to the approval completion bus independent of any specific automation rule). This projection writes approval-instance data into a dedicated multitable base/sheet unconditionally on completion, which is neither record-link, nor the start_approval trigger, nor write_approval_form_values (FWB) — a fourth, currently-implemented table<->approval interop mechanism the governing lock document itself distinguishes FWB from rather than folds into any of the three named seams. |

FOR UPDATE` on the instance row (:9553), and — for a 'handle' action, still inside that same transaction/lock, no intervening BEGIN — calls `applyHandlerFieldWrites` (:10363, defined :12193) which validates each written field against the frozen template-version schema and issues the sole `UPDATE approval_instances SET form_snapshot = COALESCE(form_snapshot,'{}'::jsonb) \|\| $2::jsonb ...` (:12296-12300, an in-place merge, not a new row), then `insertFormFieldRevisions` appends one `approval_form_field_revisions` row per changed field (called at :10440 and :10536, both before the branch's COMMIT). The FWB-2 read additionally JOINs to `approval_template_versions v ON v.id = i.template_version_id` — the instance's PINNED version, never a live/current template row — so the executor cannot read a 'current template draft' either. |

| axioms-21 | §2 table FWB v1 | TRUE | — | FWB v1 supports two application modes: create a row from main-form fields, or update a row anchored via a record-link field. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3652-3661@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3710,3762-3766@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3839-3852,3888-3891@5133df1c5 | `executeWriteApprovalFormValuesAction` parses `config.mode` via `parseFwbWriteMode` and branches: `modeParsed.mode === 'update'` dispatches to `executeWriteApprovalFormValuesUpdateAction`, else to `executeWriteApprovalFormValuesCreateAction` (lines 3652-3661). | axioms-22 | §2 table FWB v1 | TRUE | — | FWB v1 resolves the target table/record server-side and does not trust client-supplied target identifiers. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3755@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3888-3896,3968@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-write-action.ts:72@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-permission-gates.ts:63@5133df1c5 | Target resolution is entirely server-derived from stored state, not from any per-execution client payload: create mode calls `resolveFwbRuntimeMappings(query, context.sheetId, normalized.mappings)` (line 3755, `context.sheetId` coming from the automation rule/trigger, not a request body) and `resolveFwbRuntimeMappings` is defined in approval-fwb-write-action.ts:72. | axioms-23 | §2 table FWB v1 | TRUE | — | FWB v1 supports exactly the field types text, date and select for writeback. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-activation.ts:38@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-activation.ts:124-137@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-actions.ts:72@5133df1c5 | `V1_TARGET_TYPES = new Set(['text', 'number', 'date', 'select'])` (approval-fwb-activation.ts:38) is the structural mapping-target-type allowlist checked at save time, and `automation-actions.ts:72` types `targetType` as `'text' \| 'number' \| 'date' \| 'select'` — so 'number' is structurally present in the type union. However, `hasUnavailableFwbNumberMapping` (approval-fwb-activation.ts:134-137) unconditionally rejects any mapping whose `targetType === 'number'`, and this guard is invoked at both save (automation-service.ts:2191, routes/automation.ts:328) and execute (automation-executor.ts:1294, 3726, 3859) with no way to opt in. The functionally usable/effective writeback types are therefore exactly text, date, and select — matching the claim — with 'number' present in the type literal but permanently save/execute-blocked (see axioms-24). |
| axioms-24 | §2 table FWB v1 | TRUE | — | Number fields are rejected by FWB at both rule save time and execute time via a hard-coded exact-number stop rule that is not controlled by an environment flag. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-activation.ts:124-137@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-service.ts:2191@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/routes/automation.ts:328@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3726,3859@5133df1c5 | `hasUnavailableFwbNumberMapping` (approval-fwb-activation.ts:134-137) is documented as the 'Exact-number activation stop rule' with the comment: 'This is deliberately NOT an environment flag: an operator must not be able to opt into a partially implemented numeric contract. Save and execute both call this guard; D0-D4 will replace it with the ratified server capability.' It returns true whenever any mapping has `targetType === 'number'`, with no env/flag lookup in its body. It is called at rule-save time in automation-service.ts:2191 and routes/automation.ts:328, and at rule-execute time in automation-executor.ts (create action line 3726, update action line 3859, and line 1294), matching the 'save+execute double gate, no env flag' claim exactly. |

FOR UPDATE` (row-locked, :9553), then throws 409 INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION if `instance.status !== 'pending'` (:10262) before ever reaching applyHandlerFieldWrites' in-place `UPDATE ... SET form_snapshot = COALESCE(...) \|\| $2` (:12297) — all in one transaction, so this writer cannot touch a terminal row. 'Only pending requests can be edited'` (:34589) — but that check is on `attendance_requests.status`, a DIFFERENT table/column than `approval_instances.status`, and no `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on approval_instances itself was found in this path. The repo's own DML-debt inventory independently catalogs this second site as P12: 'Approval request creation/edit: pending edit overwrites mutable form_snapshot without a version' (curated-debt-entries.cjs:335), i.e. scoped to pending edits, consistent with non-terminal-only mutation, though via a proxy status field rather than approval_instances.status directly. Only a test fixture (multitable-fwb-update-activation-realdb.test.ts:773) writes form_snapshot unconditionally, and that is test setup, not production code. No writer found that updates form_snapshot for a row whose approval_instances.status is itself terminal. |

| v11-a-17 | §3 唯一权威值 | TRUE | — | In the web app, `ProductFeatures` type, `RouteFeatureGuard`'s `requiredFeature` prop, `hasFeature` helper, and the dev-mode localStorage feature override all exist and model features as booleans only. | apps/web/src/stores/featureFlags.ts:7-55@5133df1c5d; apps/web/src/stores/featureFlags.ts:58-59@5133df1c5d; apps/web/src/stores/featureFlags.ts:490@5133df1c5d; apps/web/src/stores/featureFlags.ts:163@5133df1c5d; apps/web/src/stores/featureFlags.ts:174-176@5133df1c5d | `RouteFeatureGuard.requiredFeature?: keyof Omit<ProductFeatures, 'mode'>` (:59), `hasFeature(feature: keyof Omit<ProductFeatures, 'mode'>): boolean` (:490), and `setLocalFeatureOverride(feature: keyof Omit<ProductFeatures, 'mode'>, value: boolean)` (:163) are ALL typed to accept only ProductFeatures keys minus 'mode', with boolean values — a boolean-only contract by explicit exclusion of the one non-boolean member. That member is `ProductFeatures.mode: ProductMode` (:55), a string union ('platform'\|'attendance'\|'plm-workbench'), NOT boolean — the interface (:7-55) is not itself 100% boolean; every named surface (RouteFeatureGuard, hasFeature) works around it via the same `Omit<ProductFeatures,'mode'>` pattern. The dev-mode localStorage override also carries a SEPARATE non-boolean channel for mode: `parseOverrideFeatures()` reads a distinct `metasheet_product_mode` localStorage key (:174) into a `ProductMode \| undefined`, independent of the boolean `metasheet_features` JSON blob that `setLocalFeatureOverride` writes. So the 'boolean contract' claim holds for how each of the four named surfaces treats an individual FEATURE, but only because 'mode' is uniformly special-cased/excluded, not because ProductFeatures as a whole is a pure boolean type. |

| v11-a-49 | §3 导入与行操作合同 | TRUE | — | The repository contains no zip-bomb protection test/proof for xlsx parsing. | apps/web/src/views/attendance/importXlsxConvert.ts:96-101@5133df1c5; docs/development/attendance-import-xlsx-direct-x1-verification-20260707.md:16@5133df1c5 | `git grep -ni "zip.bomb\|zipbomb\|decompression bomb\|billion laugh" 5133df1c5` (whole repo) returns exactly two hits, both prose in docs, neither a test/proof: (1) attendance-import-xlsx-direct-design-lock-20260706.md:16 — a design-doc line comparing a rejected alternative ('server-native parsing... opens the full zip-bomb/memory/async-lane surface') as a reason NOT to build server-side parsing; (2) attendance-import-xlsx-direct-x1-verification-20260707.md:16 — describes a P2-2 unit-test suite around `classifyXlsxReadError` whose 'too-large' branch is characterized parenthetically as '(arrayBuffer 未调=zip-bomb 姿态)' i.e. a file-size pre-check that rejects before ever calling `.arrayBuffer()`. Tracing that to code: `classifyXlsxReadError` (importXlsxConvert.ts:96-101) only pattern-matches error messages for 'password/encrypted/ECMA-376/agile' vs a generic 'unreadable' fallback — it contains no decompression-ratio or expanded-size check, and the actual 'too-large' guard (importXlsxConvert.ts:16-17) is a plain `file.size > IMPORT_XLSX_MAX_BYTES` compressed-file-size check, which does not detect a small file that decompresses to a huge payload (the defining zip-bomb shape) — it only rejects a large compressed upload outright. No test in the repo constructs a small-compressed/large-decompressed fixture and asserts protection. So the repo has no genuine zip-bomb protection test or proof for xlsx parsing, for approval or any other domain — confirming the assertion's 'do not falsely claim the repo already has zip-bomb proof.' |
| v11-a-50 | §3 导入与行操作合同 | TRUE | — | A multitable helper `parseXlsxBuffer` exists and stringifies all cell values. | apps/web/src/multitable/import/xlsx-mapping.ts:45-54@5133df1c5 (normalizeRowCell); apps/web/src/multitable/import/xlsx-mapping.ts:65,85,108@5133df1c5 (parseXlsxBuffer: raw:false + normalizeRowCell call); packages/core-backend/src/multitable/xlsx-service.ts:59-65@5133df1c5 (normalizeRowCell); packages/core-backend/src/multitable/xlsx-service.ts:117,175,206@5133df1c5 (parseXlsxBuffer: raw:false + normalizeRowCell call) | Both the frontend and backend `parseXlsxBuffer` call `XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(ws, {header:1, raw:false, defval:'', blankrows:false})` and then map every cell through a `normalizeRowCell` that returns a plain string for every JS type it sees (Date -> ISOString, boolean -> 'true'/'false', finite number -> String(value), non-finite number -> '', everything else -> String(value)); the function's declared return shape is `rows: string[][]`. This is lossy stringification (non-finite numbers, null/undefined all collapse to the empty string), not mere type coercion. |
| v11-a-51 | §3 导入与行操作合同 | NOT_A_MAIN_CLAIM | ②规范性目标 | Import mapping binds by column coordinate to detail child field id; headers are display-only (v1.1 target). Reproducible sweep, all empty: `git grep -rln "ApprovalDetailImport\|detailRowsFromXlsx\|importDetailRows\|detailRowImport" -- .`; `git grep -rln "xlsx" -- apps/web/src/approvals packages/core-backend/src/services/ApprovalProductService.ts`; `git log --all --oneline --grep="导入" -i` (matches are all attendance/stock-prep, none touch approval detail import). The only column-mapping code in the repo, `mapXlsxColumnsToFields`, binds by case-insensitive HEADER NAME to a top-level MetaField id, not by column coordinate to a detail child id, and multitable itself has no 'detail'/child-field concept at all — its `MetaFieldType`/`MultitableFieldType` unions list no such member. The premise (detail fields carry child field definitions with stable ids) DOES hold, but only in the unrelated approval plugin: `FormField.columns[].id` is real and is validated server-side. The quote itself is phrased as a 必须/must requirement for a not-yet-built v1.1 adapter, not a description of current origin/main behavior. |

The only mapping code with dedup logic, `mapXlsxColumnsToFields`, tracks `usedFieldIds` and silently routes a second header targeting the same field into `unmappedHeaders` (a soft skip, not a rejection/error), and it operates on top-level MetaFields, not detail-child ids. |

That route is a different subsystem (multitable top-level record import) from the approval-detail feature the quote describes, and no code anywhere rejects on cell.f for detail-row imports. |

The durable consumer adapter registry (buildConsumerAdapterRegistry) maps a handler that resolves without throwing straight to {outcome:'success'} unconditionally, and automation-durable-consumer-handlers.ts wires the 'automation-record-trigger' consumer_key directly to automationService.handleEvent. So a run() throw (e.g. after claimEventFiresLease already flipped the row to in_progress) is swallowed by handleEvent's try/catch, handleEvent still resolves, the adapter reports success, and the outer outbox_consumer row is marked done via completeConsumer. The event_fires row is left in_progress with an expiring lease; nothing else calls claimEventFiresLease again for that exact (rule_id, dedup_key) once its owning outbox row is terminal (done), and the only periodic sweep over meta_automation_event_fires (sweepEventDedupLedger, driven by fired_at) is a retention DELETE, not a resolver. VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (instance_id, rule_id, action_key, node_key, entry_epoch, application_mode) DO NOTHING`. Its production transaction is provided by AutomationService's deps.transaction (automation-service.ts:973: `poolManager.get().transaction(...)`), which is PoolManager's `transaction<T>()` (connection-pool.ts:174), whose body issues a bare `await rawClient.query('BEGIN')` (:182) with no ISOLATION LEVEL clause and no prior SET TRANSACTION/SET SESSION CHARACTERISTICS statement on that path. A repo-wide grep for default_transaction_isolation / SET SESSION CHARACTERISTICS / -c default_transaction_isolation shows the term appears only in a different subsystem (directory-sync.ts, local-directory-org.ts, org-transfer-service.ts, and their tests) where those specific transactions defensively pin `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ COMMITTED` as their first statement precisely because they don't trust the session/server default — that defensive pin is absent from the claimActionApplied/FWB write path. A server- or role-level `ALTER DATABASE/ROLE ... SET default_transaction_isolation` is a deployment-level Postgres configuration outside this repo's source and not verifiable from code either way; the verdict covers only the code path (no explicit isolation level is set anywhere between the pool connect and the claim INSERT), which is what PostgreSQL's own documented default is READ COMMITTED absent such an override. |

What the code actually does: automation-executor.ts derives `fwbEventId = \`${baseEventId}::fwb::${context.ruleId}::${actionKey}\`` (:3738), where actionKey comes from deriveActionKey (automation-action-idempotency.ts:52), a pure sha256-based function — so a per-write event id IS deterministically derived, and it is stored in the real NOT NULL `event_id` column of meta_automation_outbox (migration :68). | v2-claim-47 | 4.3 | FALSE | ①已更正(正文 v12/v13) | A 'required alert' reporting facility (alert sink whose failure blocks completion) exists in the durable delivery path. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatch-loop.ts:60@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatch-loop.ts:544@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatch-loop.ts:559@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatch-loop.ts:568@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatch-loop.ts:601@5133df1c5 | startDurableDispatchLoop throws a synchronous TypeError at :601 if observer.onUnknownConsumerKeys / onTickError / onHeartbeatError / onClaimTimePoison are not all present as functions — that is the 'required' half (a DispatchLoopObserver, documented at :544 as the 'Mandatory telemetry sink for the long-running loop', must be wired up to even start the loop). But every actual invocation of those callbacks goes through safeInvoke (:559-568), which explicitly swallows both synchronous throws and rejected promises from the callback ('a broken telemetry sink must never kill delivery or the process', :568). The module's own doc block states the same for the single-tick DispatchTickOptions form: onUnknownConsumerKeys/onHeartbeatError/onClaimTimePoison are each commented 'Best-effort — a throwing callback never fails the tick.' So the only 'required' property that exists is presence-at-start; there is no alert sink whose runtime failure blocks, fails, or delays completion of a claim/dispatch/delivery — the design is explicitly the opposite (best-effort, exception-safe, non-blocking). |

| v2-gate3-06 | §4.5 gate 3 | TRUE | — | Durable delivery consumer rows carry `attempts` and a max-attempts limit; reaching it transitions the row to `dead_letter`. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-delivery.ts:46@5133df1c5d; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-delivery.ts:86@5133df1c5d; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatcher.ts:44@5133df1c5d; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatcher.ts:151@5133df1c5d; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-dispatcher.ts:194-199@5133df1c5d | OutboxConsumerStatus includes 'pending' \| 'in_progress' \| 'done' \| 'dead_letter'. The consumer row carries a numeric `attempts` field. `DEFAULT_MAX_DELIVERY_ATTEMPTS = 8`; `claimDueConsumers` takes an optional `maxAttempts` (defaulting to that constant). The claim SQL is `SET status = CASE WHEN c.attempts >= $5::int THEN 'dead_letter' ELSE 'in_progress' END, ... attempts = CASE WHEN c.attempts >= $5::int THEN c.attempts ELSE c.attempts + 1 END, last_error = CASE WHEN c.attempts >= $5::int THEN 'max_attempts_exhausted' ELSE c.last_error END` — i.e. a row whose persisted `attempts` has reached the ceiling is poisoned to `dead_letter` at the next claim. |

There is no 'required' variant of this alert, and no repo-wide occurrence of the terms `required alert` / `requiredAlert` / `alertRequired`. So neither half of the claimed pair (a required alert that blocks completion; a CAS failure that blocks an 'outer consumer') exists on current main — existing alert hooks are explicitly the opposite (best-effort, never blocking). |

FOR UPDATE` (row-locked within the transaction) and then, before any status-changing UPDATE, checks `if (instance.status !== 'pending') throw ServiceError(..., 409, APPROVAL_ERROR_CODES.INVALID_STATUS_TRANSITION)` (or the route-level 400 'APPROVAL_STATUS_INVALID' equivalent). | v2-ident-01 | §4.4 | TRUE | — | The existing FWB write action emits a single action-level event id per action (`fwbEventId`), and both automation event-fires and the webhook path deduplicate on that event id. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3736@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3794@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-routing-manifest.ts:85@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-consumer-handlers.ts:64@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-consumer-handlers.ts:95-96@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-consumer-handlers.ts:108@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-service.ts:2554@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-event-dedup.ts:17-24@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/webhook-service.ts:317-330@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3630-3637@5133df1c5 | FWB create/update actions set the outbox row's eventId to fwbEventId (`${baseEventId}::fwb::${ruleId}::${actionKey}`, automation-executor.ts:3736/3869) via the enqueueOutbox seam. The routing manifest fans multitable.record.created out to BOTH 'automation-record-trigger' and 'webhook-event-bridge' consumers. The record-trigger handler overlays payload._eventId = event.eventId before calling AutomationService.handleEvent, which computes dedupKey = buildAutomationEventDedupKey(eventType, payload) = `${eventType}:${_eventId}` and dedupes rule firing on (ruleId, dedupKey) against meta_fwb-style event-fires ledger — i.e. keyed on the same fwbEventId. The webhook-event-bridge handler passes that same event.eventId as webhookService.deliverEvent's 3rd arg, which keys the per-(webhook, event) delivery-row idempotency claim (legacy/non-durable callers pass no eventId and get no dedup — this eventId-keyed dedup is specific to the durable-outbox consumer path). FWB write actions cannot execute at all unless AUTOMATION_DURABLE_DELIVERY_ENABLED is true (automation-executor.ts:3630-3637 hard-fails otherwise), so this durable/eventId-keyed dedup on both sinks is the only path FWB ever takes. |

| v2-ident-06 | §4.4 | TRUE | — | The parent claim helper (`claimActionApplied` / automation-action-idempotency) already carries an `applicationMode` field with a closed union that includes the value `'apply'` (currently `'apply' \| 'test_run'`, defaulting to `'apply'`). | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-action-idempotency.ts:74@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-action-idempotency.ts:105@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-write-action.ts:151@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-write-action.ts:197@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/db/migrations/zzzz20260715200000_create_fwb_action_applied.ts:44-45@5133df1c5 | `ActionClaim.applicationMode?: 'apply' \| 'test_run'` (automation-action-idempotency.ts:74); `claimActionApplied` defaults `const mode = c.applicationMode ?? 'apply'` (line 105). The caller in approval-fwb-write-action.ts passes `applicationMode: input.applicationMode ?? 'apply'` (line 197) into `claimActionApplied`. Closedness is enforced at two levels, not just TypeScript: the migration for `meta_fwb_action_applied` stamps `application_mode text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'apply'` with `CONSTRAINT fwb_action_applied_mode_valid CHECK (application_mode IN ('apply','test_run'))` — a DB-level closed union, so a rogue value cannot be persisted even bypassing the TS type. |
| v2-ident-07 | §4.4 | TRUE | — | Current main has exactly two FWB application modes, create and update, and both call sites compute `fwbEventId` with the same `::` template. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/approval-fwb-activation.ts:50-55@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3653-3661@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3736@5133df1c5; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3869@5133df1c5 | `parseFwbWriteMode(raw)` (approval-fwb-activation.ts:50-55) enumerates exactly three raw inputs to exactly two modes: `undefined → 'create'`, `'create' → 'create'`, `'update' → 'update'`, anything else → `{ok:false}` (fails the action, no third mode reachable). The executor's dispatcher (automation-executor.ts:3653-3661) routes `mode === 'update'` to `executeWriteApprovalFormValuesUpdateAction` and everything else (i.e. 'create') to `executeWriteApprovalFormValuesCreateAction`. Both call sites compute `fwbEventId` with the byte-identical `${baseEventId}::fwb::${ruleId}::${actionKey}` template (lines 3736, 3869). This is a distinct axis from `applicationMode` ('apply'\|'test_run', v2-ident-06's field) — 'write mode' (create/update) and 'application mode' (apply/test_run) are two separate closed unions in this code, not aliases of each other. |

| v2-pos-05 | §4.5 定位 | TRUE | — | A boot / strict status check exists that fails on illegal flag combinations (dependency violations). | packages/core-backend/src/index.ts:3663-3664@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-activation.ts:117-119@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-activation.ts:136-146@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; scripts/ops/multitable-global-history-flag-status.mjs:11-17@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; scripts/ops/global-history-flag-manifest.mjs:620-654@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba | Two independent mechanisms both fail on illegal combinations. (1) packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-durable-activation.ts: durableBootFailureDisposition() and assertDurableRuntimeDependency() make process startup abort (fail-closed) when AUTOMATION_DURABLE_DELIVERY_ENABLED=true and a required runtime dependency is unavailable; index.ts's boot code (:3663-3664, :3746-3747) consults this disposition and rethrows to abort startup. (2) scripts/ops/multitable-global-history-flag-status.mjs's --strict mode, backed by global-history-flag-manifest.mjs's evaluateFlagRules(), evaluates every requires/conflicts rule across the Global History flag set and turns any illegal combination into an unconditional STOP (in both --strict and default mode). |
| v2-pos-06 | §4.5 定位 | TRUE | — | The existing FWB write action fires only on approval completion with approved outcome, not on draft or rejected instances. | packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3673-3703@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3760-3762@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-executor.ts:3844-3903@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba; packages/core-backend/src/multitable/automation-service.ts:218-236@5133df1c5d9f79bc140cb3a29cff0d623174a6ba | executeWriteApprovalFormValuesAction dispatches to a create path (executeWriteApprovalFormValuesCreateAction, mode absent/'create') and an update path (executeWriteApprovalFormValuesUpdateAction, mode 'update'); both call the shared parseFwbCompletionContext (automation-executor.ts:3673), whose 'D1 hard gate' (:3697-3703) returns a skipped result with skipReason 'fwb_outcome_not_approved' when the completion event's outcome (transition.toStatus / eventType) is not 'approved' — checked before either path runs. Both paths' own SQL additionally re-filter `status = 'approved'` when reading form_snapshot (create path :3761, update path :3882/:3903). Completion events are emitted only for terminal transitions in APPROVAL_COMPLETION_TRIGGER_EVENT_TYPES = ['approval.approved','approval.rejected','approval.revoked','approval.cancelled'] (automation-service.ts:233-236); a draft (non-terminal) instance never produces a completion event at all, so the action structurally cannot fire on a draft instance either. |
